# Record 20261011-214916-183a3fc

- **Record ID**: 20261011-214916-183a3fc
- **Experiment**: `brownout-top` — Assembled temp_por_top brown-out behavior across dip depth, hold duration, falling slew and recovery slew over the PVT grid
- **Claim**: Issue #116 (child of #106) -- NOT a spec claim: no ratified numeric brown-out envelope exists (DR-003 Sec8; spec/porting-plan.md Sec3.4 defines only the SHAPE of the three-axis sweep). Records, for the unchanged assembled design/temp_por_top.sch as committed in design/netlist/temp_por_top.spice (bias_core + temp_core + por_comparator + por_output_chain, real shared IBIAS node, real RESETn -> temp_core.EN feedback, no ideal bias or sensor substitution, PTAT/CTAT open), what RESETn does across an exploratory set of single supply dips (floor x hold x falling slew, plus recovery-slew variants and a no-dip control) x {tt,ss,ff,sf,fs} x {-40,27,125 C} x {2.97,3.30,3.63 V} starting supplies, graded from full-resolution RESETn/POR_RAW/VDD/IBIAS waveforms and the explicit delivered bias branch current (sim/brownout-top/brownout_checker.py). A completed simulation is not a behavioural PASS. gf180mcu numbers are not used anywhere; failed cells are evidence.

## Measurement definitions (exploratory; none is a ratified bound)
- **levels**: V_IL = 0.2 vf, V_IH = 0.8 vf, V_MID = 0.5 vf, V_MUST = 0.5 vf (vf = the point's starting supply)
- **assertion**: RESETn falls through V_IL starting from HIGH at t0 (Schmitt).  assertion_rail_limited is recorded when VDD itself was <= V_IL at that instant (the output could not stay high regardless of the comparator).  Release = RESETn rises through V_IH.
- **detectability**: a dip is 'required-to-assert' when VDD spends >= 50 us below V_MUST (piecewise-linear dwell from the full-resolution VDD).  Dips that are not required may assert or not -- both are recorded, neither fails -- but an assertion must still recover.
- **baseline**: viable when, before t0: exactly one RESETn release and no re-assert, RESETn >= V_IH over the 2 ms before t0, delivered bias branch current > 0 with peak-to-peak <= 5 % of its mean over that window.  Otherwise UNRESOLVED (the dip response cannot be attributed to the dip); detected codes are kept in 'detected_codes_ungraded'.
- **recovery**: VDD recovered when >= 0.98 vf after the recovery edge starts; an assertion beginning later is reassert_after_recovery; two or more assertions are reassert_chatter; RESETn must be >= V_IH over the last 1 ms.
- **delivered bias**: explicit measurement: drain current [id] of bias_core XMPIB (PMOS whose drain IS the IBIAS node), saved by the testbench as .save @m.xdut.xbias.xmpib.msky130_fd_pr__pfet_g5v0d10v5[id] and appearing in the klt waveform as i(@m.xdut.xbias.xmpib.msky130_fd_pr__pfet_g5v0d10v5[id]).  Sign: ngspice BSIM4 reports id POSITIVE for normal conduction in both polarities (verified by a local single-corner probe: XMPIB id ~0.78 uA equals the sum of the comparator, output-chain and temp_core diode devices' ids ~0.39+0.39+0 uA at 1.5 ms), so the delivered branch current is +id and must be positive at baseline (else NONPHYSICAL).  The consumer-side diode devices (comparator, output chain, temp_core XMBD [id]) are saved; their sum over the delivered current at the end is recorded (sink_sum_over_delivered_end).  The IBIAS NODE voltage v(xdut.ibias) is recorded separately and never used as a current.  End/baseline ratio band [0.8, 1.25] else FAIL bias_not_recovered.
- **resolution**: max sample gap from t0-10 us to VDD-recovery+100 us must be <= min(20 us, hold/5, shortest derived edge/10), AND sampled VDD must match the declared stimulus within 20 mV; otherwise a point with no detected failure is UNRESOLVED, not PASS.
- **physicality**: every saved node within [-0.3 V, vf+0.5 V] for t>0, settled supply current -i(BVDD) > 0, delivered bias positive at baseline.  Violation -> NONPHYSICAL (cannot PASS; behavioural verdict not graded).
- **verdicts**: ERROR (missing waveform / channel / non-finite data / convergence, model or backend error) > NONPHYSICAL > UNRESOLVED (unviable baseline, truncated, sparse, stimulus mismatch) > FAIL (resolved physical failure, with trace evidence) > PASS.  NOT_COVERED marks points whose request was never submitted, was refused by the fleet, or has no report; they are counted as uncovered, never as PASS.
- **stimulus**: V(VDD) = min(vf, rup*t) - ex*( clip01((t-t0)*sf/ex) - clip01((t-t0-ex/sf-th)*sr/ex) ), ex = max(vf - vlow, 0).  Four independent axes: floor vlow, falling slew sf, hold th, recovery slew sr (+ t0).  The fall lasts ex/sf and the recovery ex/sr: durations are DERIVED from voltage excursion / slew, never a fixed duration, so the swept starting supply does not couple into either slew.  Implemented in the testbench BVDD source (SPICE twin of brownout_checker.vdd_expected, which the checker uses to verify the sampled VDD).
- **observation**: 10 ms after the recovery edge ends (tstop = t0 + fall + hold + recovery + 10 ms, taken at the largest starting supply): >2.3x the slowest observed release delay (4.3 ms).  A shorter record is truncated -> UNRESOLVED unless a failure is already detected.
- **grid**: Floors: 2.0 V is a shallow brown-out above half supply (never 'required-to-assert' under the declared detectability rule), 1.2 V is below half supply, 0.4 V is below V_IL at every starting supply (rail collapse).  Holds span 10 us - 1 ms (two decades).  Falling slews 2 kV/s and 50 kV/s (a ~2 V excursion in ~1 ms and ~40 us) -- two levels a factor 25 apart, kept few because simulation cost scales with 1/tmax and the first 3-level grid timed out on the fleet.  Faster slews (100 kV/s ... 1 MV/s need tmax <= 50 ns) are the first declared expansion (policy b).  Recovery slew is held at 10 kV/s for the full floor x hold x fall cube and varied one-at-a-time (1 kV/s, 100 kV/s) around the center point only -- a declared one-factor-at-a-time treatment of the fourth axis, not a full 4-D cube.
- **expansion policy**: No envelope is claimed.  Expansion is a NEW record, never an edit: (a) if a boundary (PASS<->FAIL) appears between adjacent grid levels on any axis, add the midpoint level on that axis only at the affected corners; (a') a middle falling slew (e.g. 10 kV/s) and the 100 kV/s class are added next; (b) falling or recovery slews above 100 kV/s (first: 1 MV/s) need tmax <= 50 ns and a per-request cost estimate (the fleet1 timeout shows cost is binding) before they are added; (c) holds above 1 ms or below 10 us and floors between the three levels are added only with a stated reason; (d) the passive-skew (ll/hh) and mismatch axes are not in this increment.  Any post-hoc addition is labelled as such in its record, as in the #142 diagnosis.
- **trace storage**: committed traces are change-triggered (dV >= 2 mV on any of VDD/RESETn/POR_RAW/IBIAS node, delivered bias moved >= 2 % of its maximum, or a 1 ms anchor); RESETn assertion/release counts are re-derived from the stored trace and compared with the full-resolution result per point.

## Absent coverage (stated, not hidden)
- The initial dip grid is exploratory and bounded (see experiment.json grid.not_covered); no brown-out envelope is claimed or ratified (DR-003 Sec8). The expansion policy is declared in the manifest.
- Recovery slew is varied one-factor-at-a-time around the center point only, not crossed with the full floor x hold x fall cube.
- The orthogonal ll/hh passive-skew axis and mismatch Monte Carlo are not exercised; PTAT/CTAT are open; no post-layout parasitics.
- temp_core startup artifacts of #86 are not presumed fixed: an affected point shows up as an UNRESOLVED baseline, a NONPHYSICAL node, or an ERROR in the table, not as a PASS.
- POR_RAW-referenced and VDD-level GLITCH characterization is the sibling child of #106 and is not exercised here.

## Notes
**This is the tightened-solver PILOT record, not the full re-grade.** Under the cost rule declared in `experiment.json` `tightened_rerun.cost_rule`, two pilot requests (`tightened_rerun.pilot_tags`) were submitted first. Both reached the fleet's whole-job limit. Under that rule the other 19 requests were **not submitted**, so they appear as NOT_COVERED. No tightened point is graded, and the brown-out behavior at the 673 source-NONPHYSICAL points is still **unknown**.

Pilot jobs:

- `control`: job `klt-sim-c3ba332c37d3`, c7i.4xlarge spot, state `timeout`, 3605 s. The default deck took 450 s for the same request (job `klt-sim-c07d9e896014`).
- `cube_v1.2_h0.0001_f50000_r10000`: job `klt-sim-fd2409da90fb`, c7i.4xlarge spot, state `timeout`, 3604 s. The default deck took 1402 s (job `klt-sim-7e1eaffffee5`).

Both pilot jobs ran on runner klt 0.5.0 (client 0.7.0+g04ca88ce427c, `batch.runner_version_check=warn`). The harness log of both jobs reads `running (timeout 3600s)`, so the enforced per-job limit was **3600 s**, not the 7200 s assumed in `tightened_rerun.job_limit_s`. The limit discarded every corner of each job, including corners that had finished (2AMLogic/klayout-tools#2833). All 90 pilot points are therefore ERROR (`batch_job_timeout`) under the existing accounting. They are not UNRESOLVED, because no waveform came back.

Refusal accounting: before the pilots launched, each pilot request was refused five times by the shared fleet's `BATCH_MAX_CONCURRENT_INSTANCES=8` cap. klt's `capacity_wait_s` does not wait out that refusal (2AMLogic/klayout-tools#2917), so `run --slot-wait` resubmitted every 300 s. Each refusal is archived and listed per request as an earlier attempt.

Post-hoc diagnosis (not graded, not part of the declared grading):

- The S3 prefix of each timed-out job still holds the per-corner ngspice logs. For `control`, 20 of 45 corners had finished when the limit fired. Of the 8 still running, 7 were 125 C corners. For `cube_v1.2_h0.0001_f50000_r10000`, 10 of 45 had finished. These partial results are not graded: the job report was lost, and grading S3 scraps would bypass the declared harness.
- Two single-corner LOCAL timing probes (`probe --fleet-equivalent`: no `ngspice_init`, so the default SPARSE solver, as on the fleet) ran `control` at ff / 125 C / 2.97 V. The default deck took 19 s (2062 rows). The `reltol2e-4` deck took 389 s (148270 rows). That is about **20x per corner**. The fleet took 90 s for this corner on the default deck, so a whole-request job of the tightened deck projects far past 3600 s. Even one-corner jobs of the slowest default corners (up to 541 s on the fleet) would project past it.
- The same local tightened probe ended with the settled supply current reversed (-1.8 mA at the end). That is the NONPHYSICAL signature, at a point the default deck graded physical PASS. This is a single local debug probe, not a recorded verdict. It does show that the tightened solver can also move a control point *onto* the nonphysical branch.

The projection above applies the pilots' ratio (a lower bound, x>=8.0 on whole-job elapsed time) to every request's default-deck elapsed time. Against the enforced 3600 s limit, all 21 requests project over it. Before any further fleet submission of this variant, a declared decision is needed. Options include: carry `option klu` in the netlist (the repo `sim/spiceinit` sets it, but the fleet runner ignores `ngspice_init`, so every fleet run so far used SPARSE); split requests to one corner per job; run on a fleet with a longer job limit; or declare a less tight tolerance. Any of these is a new declared manifest change, not an edit to this record.

The default-deck record `20261010-222529-41bde9f` is unchanged and remains the evidence for the default deck.
